#2879. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2873. Eagles continue reshaping roster by reportedly releasing veteran wide receiver

Eagles continue reshaping roster by reportedly releasing veteran wide receiver originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Hollywood Brown's brief and odd Eagles career is over after four games and five catches.

The Eagles are releasing Brown by mutual consent of the player and the team, Jordan Schultz of the Scultz Report posted on social media Thursday night.

The Eagles signed Brown to a one-year, $5 million contract in March with the expectation that he'd be a significant part of the Eagles' passing game following the departure of A.J. Brown. But even with Brown gone and DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks both hurt much of the season, Brown has been an afterthought.

He's only played in four of the Eagles' nine games, getting nine targets and catching five passes for 63 yards along with two rushing attempts for 12 yards. His longest catch was a 19-yarder from Tanner McKee in the loss to the Cowboys. He had a season-high 30 yards in that game.

Brown expressed frustration with his limited role in a recent conversation with NBC Sports Philadelphia:

"Of course, you want to be somewhere where you play, but at the ultimate end of the day, it's not my plan. So I might want to play, I might want to contribute, but if that's not the plan for me, I can't dwell on it too much or you'll be unhappy. And then you're taking that back home. I can't take that back home to the wife and kids. So I gotta try to stay as positive as I can."

Asked how that was going, he laughed and said, "Some days better than others."

He added: "We're all dealing with something and we're dealing with something as a team. So I don't wanna add that extra stress."

Brown said he expected to play more but was dealing with it because he didn't want to put personal issues ahead of team needs.

"I did (expect a bigger role), but I do understand stuff happens," he said. "I mean, you can't take anything personal in this business, so you kind of just got to go about your process and when that time happens, whatever happens, you've just got to be ready for it."

Brown, a 1st-round pick in 2019, had a 1,000-yard season for the Ravens in 2021 and over 700 yards in both 2020 and 2022. He was 49-for-587 with five touchdowns last year. In his career, he has 378 catches for 4,385 yards and 33 touchdowns.

The Eagles, 2-7, have lost seven straight game. Their offense is ranked 32nd in the NFL in yards per game, 30th in points per game and 29th in passing yards per game.

With Brown no longer on the roster, the Eagles have rookie 1st-round pick Makai Lemon, 2nd-year undrafted free agent Darius Cooper and Elijah Moore as healthy wide receivers. Smith and Wicks have both missed the last two games but could be back after the bye week.

Lemon is the Eagles' leading receiver with 37-for-312 and a touchdown and Smith (26-310-2), Wicks (19-284-1) and Cooper (13-200-4) are next.

The Eagles have two WRs on the practice squad – Erik Ezukanma and Xavier Gipson.

Because it's after the trade deadline, Brown is subject to the waiver claim process despite being a vested veteran with seven years of pension credit.

#2870. Roob's Eagles Observations: Time to turn to a Stout disciple who's already here?

Roob's Eagles Observations: Time to turn to a Stout disciple who's already here? originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Could changing offensive line coaches help? How dumb are the Bengals? Did the Eagles do Joe Milton a disservice starting him so soon?

All good questions and we have some answers to those and several other questions in this weekend's 10 Random Observations as the Eagles give all of us a break from their ineptitude at least for one Sunday.

COULD GREG AUSTIN HELP? I don't believe that all the offensive line's problems are the fault of new o-line coach Chris Kuper. Lane Johnson's retirement and chronic injuries to Landon Dickerson and pre-trade Cam Jurgens sure didn't help. But things like the communication issues, Tyler Steen's regression and the inability of this group to play the brand of smart, physical play we're used to around here are squarely on Kuper, and it's been bad. The offensive line should be playing a lot better than it is, even without Lane, and they've been so bad they really haven't given whoever's playing quarterback a chance. It was pretty damning to hear Drew Kendall say, "We all got to get on the same page from a communication standpoint," when we're halfway through the season. I don't think there's any chance the Eagles make a change. That's not how they do things. Nonetheless, I am curious whether moving his responsibilities over to assistant offensive line coach Greg Austin might help. Here's what I like about Austin: He worked alongside Jeff Stoutland from 2013 through 2015 after following Chip Kelly here from Oregon, where he was a quality control guy under Chip. Austin had a number of other jobs – including under Doug Pederson in Jacksonville – before rejoining the Eagles in 2025 and getting one last year in with Stout. I like the Stout connection and if you can't bring Stout back – which obviously the Eagles will never do as long as Nick Sirianni is head coach – how about bringing back a discipline of Stout, a guy who understands how he coached and taught? Now, the big issue here is that everything Kuper does with offensive linemen – the way they line up, the way they block, the way they communicate (or otherwise) – is different than how Stout did things. It's probably too late to go back to Stout's scheme sitting here in Week 10, but if Austin can channel some of that Stout wisdom and experience and apply it to what this line is already trying to do, maybe that would help? Sure as heck can't hurt.

HOW DUMB ARE THE BENGALS? The Arnold Ebiketie situation is literally unbelievable. How did the Bengals not understand the ramifications that trading for Ebiketie would have on the comp pick formula? How is that even possible? The Bengals were all set to send the Eagles a 6th-round pick Tuesday for Ebiketie – why they even wanted him in the first place is another mystery – but pulled the plug on the trade after seeing a tweet from Over the Cap with comp pick expert Nick Korte explaining that the trade would nullify the 3rd-round comp pick they were due for losing Trey Hendrickson in free agency. How does Bengals de facto GM Duke Tobin not know the rules? How can a dude working for a website – a very sharp dude – know more about the comp pick formula than a guy running an NFL team? And why would the Bengals want to trade a pick in the first place for an edge rusher who has no sacks, no tackles for loss and one hurry in 300 snaps this year with the Eagles? The Bengals are 6-2 and they've won four straight so they're doing something right. But it's wild to think that a guy from Over the Cap saved them from losing a 3rd-round pick by acquiring Arnold Ebiketie of all people. Then they make head coach Zac Taylor hold the press conference to discuss the situation while Tobin hides out in his office. The Bengals should probably fire Tobin and hire Korte.

MILTON JUST WASN'T READY: I said it before the game so this isn't second guessing, but there was no way Joe Milton could have been ready to face the Commanders just 23 days after the first time he walked into the Eagles' facility and with just 10 practices under his belt. You can't even begin to get comfortable in an NFL offense that quickly. Considering everything, I thought Milton played as well as you could possibly hope for. This is not just a guy new in this offense, he was also making his first NFL start. Too much to overcome. Andy Reid used to say it takes a quarterback five years in the same offense to truly master it. Joe Milton didn't even have five weeks. There's a reason there are OTAs in the spring, throwing sessions with the receivers during down time in the summer and then five weeks of training camp. All that stuff matters. If you're going to play the most challenging position on the field you need that time to truly get comfortable in the offense, to understand how your receivers run routes, to get a feel for how your offensive linemen block (or don't) and to get a sense for how the play caller – good or bad – calls plays. Milton had none of that. The Eagles sent him out there unprepared and that didn't make sense for anybody.

PUTTING ALL THESE DRAFT PICKS IN PERSPECTIVE: Howie Roseman's current draft stockpile is two 1st-round picks and a 2nd-round pick in 2027, two 1st-round picks and two 2nd-round picks in 2028 and a 1st-round pick and two 2nd-round picks in 2029. Keeping in mind that Roseman will use some of these picks to draft players and others as trade capital, let's put that into perspective:

The Eagles have never had 10 picks in the first two rounds over a three-year period. The most they've had is nine from 1993 through 1995 – four 1sts and five 2nds. Those drafts produced Lester Holmes, Leonard Renfro, Bernard Williams and Mike Mamula in the first round and Victor Bailey, Bruce Walker, Charlie Garner, Bobby Taylor and Barrett Brooks;

The Eagles have never had five 1st-round picks in a three-year period. The most they've had is four from 1948 through 1950, producing Clyde Scott, Chuck Bednarik, Frank Tripucka and Bud Grant, and 1971 through 1973, producing Richard Harris, John Reaves, Jerry Sisemore and Charle Young.

The Eagles have also never had back-to-back drafts with multiple 1st-round picks. They had two in 1949, 1973, 1993 and 2023. The last time they had two 1sts and a 2nd was 1993.

There have only been nine times the Eagles have had two 1sts and a 2nd: 1983, 1986, 1994, 1995, 1996, 2000, 2002, 2005 and 2019.

"A-TISKET, A WHAT?????" It was 1938. Joe DiMaggio was A.L. MVP. FDR was President. The most popular song in the U.S. was "A-Tisket, A-Tasket," by Chick Webb and his Orchestra. That's also the last time before this year that the Eagles were outgained by at least 300 yards through nine games both rushing and passing. The Eagles have been out-rushed by 338 yards and out-passed by 501 yards. In 1938, they were out-rushed by 640 yards and out-passed by 604 yards. It's been a while.

BARRY, JOSH ALLEN, JONATHAN TAYLOR AND JALEN: This is kind of crazy, but Jalen Hurts has the 4th-most rushing touchdowns in NFL history among players who only played for one team. Hurts ranks 40th with 64 career rushing touchdowns, but 36 of the 39 guys ahead of him played for multiple teams. Just goes to show you how rare in the free agency era for a guy to spend his entire career with one team. The top three on that list are Barry Sanders (99), who only played for the Lions, Josh Allen (88) with the Bills, and Salem's Jonathan Taylor (77), who looked like he might be moving at the trade deadline but has still only played for the Colts. What would the all-time top-10 most rushing TDs by guys who only played for one team look like? 1) Sanders, 2) Allen, 3) Taylor, 4) Hurts, 5) Alvin Kamara of the Saints (63), 6) Terrell Davis of the Broncos (60), 7) Tampa's Mike Alstott (58), 8) Tiki Barber with the Giants (55), 9), Neal Anderson with the Bears (51) and 10) Rodney Hampton of the Giants, Deuce McAllister of the Saints and Washington's John Riggins (all 49). Among Eagles running backs, only four had at least 10 rushing touchdowns without ever playing for another team: Steve Van Buren (69), Anthony Toney (14), Heath Sherman (10) and Frank Ziegler (10). I may be the only one who finds that fascinating.

MAKAI LEMON STAT OF THE WEEK: With seven catches against the Cowboys and six each against the Commanders and Giants, Makai Lemon became the first Eagles rookie ever with six catches in three consecutive games. DeSean Jackson had five catches in four straight games as a rookie in 2008.

WHAT ON EARTH HAPPENED TO CARSON WENTZ? I guess it's just the accumulation of injuries on a 33-year-old quarterback, but, man, it was sad watching Carson Wentz play Monday night against the Bills. He was so good back in the day. And not just in 2017 but for much of 2018 and 2019 and even most of 2021 with the Colts. From 2017 through 2021 he had 124 touchdown passes (8th-most in the league) and 43 interceptions. He was still functional at times after that and had some decent starts in his various stops – Washington, Rams, Chiefs and now Vikings – but what we saw Monday night was horrifying. Wentz looked hopeless. He couldn't throw, he couldn't move, his decision making was atrocious, his pocket awareness was absent. Wentz's 8.9 passer rating Monday night is lowest by an NFL starting quarterback in three years and lowest by a player drafted by the Eagles since Donovan McNabb had a 0.4 rating in a game against the Dolphins in 2007. Wentz turns 34 next month, and he's running out of teams. Monday night was his 101st career start and I'm guessing it will wind up as the last one. Crazy career, from MVP candidate and key piece of a Super Bowl champion to hapless journeyman. Sad to see. But no Eagles quarterback was ever better than Wentz the first 14 weeks of 2017. He was 11-2 with 33 touchdowns, seven INTs and a 101.9 passer rating. Without Wentz there is no No. 1 seed and without a No. 1 seed there probably is no parade up Broad Street and for that Wentz should always be remembered fondly by Eagles fans.

CAN THE EAGLES LOSE OUT? How rare would it be for the Eagles to lose out after opening the season 2-0? Incredibly rare. Only one team in the last 84 years has won its first two games and not won another. That was the 2013 Texans – a team covered by Dave Zangaro for CSN Houston – who beat the Chargers and Titans to open the season, then lost 14 straight by an average of 11 ½ points to finish 2-14. The only other teams to lose out after a 2-0 start were the 1924 Dayton Triangles, who beat the Frankford Yellow Jackets and Buffalo Bisons before losing their last eight games to finish 2-8, and the 1941 Cleveland Rams, who opened with wins over the Steelers and Cardinals before losing their last 10 and finishing 2-10. Since 1942, 471 teams have opened a season 2-0, and 470 of them have won at least one more game the rest of the season. Can the Eagles really lose their last 15 games? Have you watched them lately?

THEY'RE REALLY BAD ON 4TH DOWN: Another victim of the Eagles' offensive malaise has been fourth down. They're terrible at it. From 2021 through 2025, the Eagles were 3rd-best in the NFL on fourth down, converting 63 percent on 82-for-130. This year, they're tied for 27th at 33.3 percent on 6-for-18. That's their 6th-worst percentage through nine games since the NFL began tracking fourth downs in 1991. Nobody likes going for it on fourth down more than Nick Sirianni, but this year has been a 4th-down nightmare. During the seven-game losing streak, the Eagles are 5-for-17 on fourth down for 29.4 percent, their worst seven-game span in seven years. Whose fault is this? Usual suspects. Sirianni, Sean Mannion, Chris Kuper, offensive line. Take your pick.

For release at 4:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation and the Federal Reserve Board today published feedback letters for resolution plans submitted in October 2025. The agencies did not identify any shortcomings or deficiencies in these resolution plan submissions.

Resolution plans, also known as living wills, describe a banking organization's strategy for orderly resolution in the event of material financial distress or failure. The agencies conducted a joint review of the 2025 resolution plan submissions from 15 banking organizations with over $250 billion in assets.

In addition, the agencies determined that the shortcoming previously identified in the 2021 BNP Paribas resolution plan has been satisfactorily addressed.

Lo staff medico ha fermato per precauzione anche Hendry Blank e ha autorizzato entrambi a tornare ai rispettivi club.

Nicolò Tresoldi salterà la sfida della Germania Under 21 contro il Belgio e tornerà al Bruges dopo aver riportato un trauma cranico in nazionale. Secondo quanto riportato da SportMediaset, L’attaccante si è scontrato testa contro testa con il compagno Hendry Blank durante la rifinitura.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il contatto è avvenuto in un contrasto aereo. Il ct Antonio Di Salvo ha confermato l’episodio alla vigilia della partita con il Belgio: Tresoldi, che nell’azione era riuscito a segnare, ha accusato mal di testa dopo l’impatto. Blank ha invece riportato una commozione cerebrale.

Le condizioni dei due giocatori non destano particolari preoccupazioni. Lo staff medico ha comunque disposto uno stop precauzionale per gestire le conseguenze dello scontro: entrambi sono stati esclusi dalla gara e autorizzati a rientrare nei rispettivi club. SportMediaset riferisce che Tresoldi lascerà così il ritiro della nazionale giovanile tedesca.

#2865. Federal Reserve Board finalizes changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 9:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Wednesday finalized changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements. The two final rules are largely similar to the proposed rules from 2025. The Board also requested comment on a proposal to better capture differences in banks' business models to generate fee income.

The Board conducts stress tests to ensure that large banks are sufficiently capitalized and able to lend to households and businesses even in a severe recession. In December 2024, the Board announced it would modify the test to improve its resilience.

"The stress test is an essential component of our regulatory capital framework," said Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle W. Bowman. "Today's changes preserve its resilience by ensuring that it is transparent, granular, and risk-sensitive. The public will now have greater assurance that the risks banks take will be reflected appropriately in their stress test losses and their capital requirements."

The first final rule requires the Board to invite public input annually on the stress test scenarios and any material model changes. It also updates the framework that guides the design of the hypothetical scenarios and adopts the models that will be used for the 2027 stress test. In addition, the final rule makes several adjustments to the stress test calendar. The final rule also updates the global market shock component, which is a set of shocks to specific market components applied to banks with large trading books. These banks will now be tested against two global market shock components each year, and the Board will use the shock that produces the largest losses for each firm to calculate the firm's stress test results.

The second final rule requires the Board, when calculating stress capital buffer requirements, to average the results from the two most recent annual supervisory stress tests for firms subject to the stress test in both years. The Board will begin averaging stress capital buffer requirements in 2028 to ensure that only models incorporating public input are used in the calculation.

The proposal would request public comment on a revision to the Board's noninterest income model that would improve the stress test's ability to capture business model diversity across firms. If adopted, these adjustments would replace the current noninterest income model that projects each bank's fee income under stress. Comments are due 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

Together, the changes are likely to reduce year-over-year volatility in capital requirements by approximately 50 percent and are not expected to materially affect aggregate capital requirements.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

#2864. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2863. Warning to NFL general managers: Howie Roseman is about to fleece you

Warning to NFL general managers: Howie Roseman is about to fleece you originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

A broken-down receiver, a washed-up guard and an underachieving defensive tackle walk into a bar. And Howie Roseman walks out with two 1st-round picks and three 2nd-round picks.

If I were an NFL general manager, I wouldn't even answer the phone when Howie calls. Because you're going to get fleeced.

And if there's one thing to feel good about during this miserable season to nowhere, it's the way Howie has stockpiled premium draft picks with a series of what sure seem like lopsided trades.

It all started in June when Roseman shipped disgruntled and divisive A.J. Brown to the Patriots for a 1st-round pick in 2027 and a 5th-round pick in 2027.

Brown was one of the best in the league not too long ago. He owns the two-best receiving seasons in Eagles history – 88-for-1,496 and 11 TDs in 2022 and 106-for-1,456-7 in 2023.

But it sure seems like his knees won't allow him to be that kind of player again. The A.J. Brown we saw hobbling around and dropping passes in his final game in an Eagles uniform, the playoff loss to the 49ers in January, seems to be closer to the player he is now than the Pro Bowler of a few years ago.

The Patriots got a WR whose balky knees have allowed him to play only three games so far this year, with just nine catches for 101 yards, three drops and a fumble.

Of 95 WRs who've been targetted at least 20 times so far this year, Brown's 15 percent drop percentage is highest. And his catch percentage is 89th. Maybe he gets healthy the second half of the season and reverts back to that three-time Pro Bowler. Or maybe this is just who A.J. Brown is right now. Either way the Eagles got a 1st-round pick for a guy who had no future in Philadelphia, didn't want to be here and may be a shot fighter at 29.

Then came the Cam Jurgens trade, and it's still hard to believe the Ravens donated the Eagles a 2nd-round pick for a guy who just hasn't been the same player as he was in 2024. Jurgens was a tremendous interior lineman before his career was derailed by injuries. Although he made a second straight Pro Bowl last year, he was only a shell of the player he was in 2023 and 2024. This year, he struggled both at center and guard before Howie someone finagled a 2nd-round pick out of the Ravens for him.

How has it gone in Baltimore for Jurgens? You always take Pro Football Focus grades with a grain of salt, but out of 89 interior linemen who've played at least 300 snaps his 43.9 combined grade with the Eagles and Ravens is 4th-worst.

In Jurgens' three games in Baltimore, the Ravens – with both Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley hurt – have scored 33 points, gone 0-3, losing 35-13 to the Bengals, 36-10 to the Bills and 33-10 to the Jaguars after starting the season 4-1.

Then there's Jordan Davis. He was a good player here and at times a very good player. He probably should have made the Pro Bowl last year. But overall? As the 13th pick in the 2022 draft and the 10th-highest paid interior lineman in the league? He's underachieved, in particular this year. He was a key starter on two Super Bowl teams but he just never dominated the way you wanted him to, the way he's being paid to. And he played just 52 percent of the snaps in his nine games this year, which ranks 27th among starting interior linemen so far this year.

Great guy, natural leader, inspiring backstory, solid lineman. But hardly indispensable. Not when you also have Jalen Carter under contract and Moro Ojomo now in line for an offseason extension. The Eagles don't really have a third high-level rotational defensive tackle now – Ty Robinson, a 4th-round pick last year, will likely take over the role. But if you can get 1st- and 2nd-round draft picks for essentially a part-time player that's a no-brainer.

Add it up and Roseman got 1st-round picks in 2027 and 2028, 2nd-round picks in 2027, 2028 and 2029 and a couple 2027 5th-round picks to boot for a beat-up wide receiver, a fading interior lineman and a defensive tackle who hasn't lived up to expectations.

The way Roseman drafts, most of those picks are going to turn into important pieces as the Eagles build back toward respectability.

There hasn't been a whole lot to celebrate on the field this year, and everything points to more of the same over these last eight games.

But up on the second floor of the Jefferson Health Training Complex, Roseman is doing all he can to make sure it doesn't happen again.

#2856. Lions, Jets renegotiated Andre Cisco trade after concerns arose about his physical

A controversy arose after the NFL trade deadline when the Broncos demanded to change the terms of their trade with the Chargers after linebacker Daiyan Henley's physical. But Henley wasn't the only traded player whose physical raised concerns.

The Lions also told the Jets they wanted to change the terms of their trade for safety Andre Cisco after concerns about his physical. But unlike the Chargers, the Jets agreed to it, and the Lions ultimately sent the Jets a 2029 sixth-round pick for Cisco after initially agreeing to give up a 2028 sixth-round pick.

Tom Pelissero reports that the modified terms were based on medical concerns. There's no word on what those concerns were, but Lions coach Dan Campbell said the plan is for Cisco to play on Sunday against the Patriots.

The Jets went along with the Lions' request, and in the scheme of things the difference between a 2028 sixth-round pick and a 2029 sixth-round pick is minor. Still, it points to an inherent flaw in the NFL's trade rules: The team acquiring a player has the ability to go back on the trade, or negotiate better terms, after the trade deadline. That puts the team trading the player in a difficult position: After you've already told a player you're trading him, and told another player you're signing him or bringing him up from the practice squad to take his place, you don't want to go back on that. So if the team you're trading with tells you, "We didn't like his physical, so we'll back out of the trade unless you take less compensation," you may feel you have no choice but to accept.

The NFL could resolve that by not allowing trades to be renegotiated after the deadline: Either the player passes the physical and the trade goes through, or the player fails the physical and the trade does not go through. No more half measures like, "We failed him on his physical, but we'll pass the physical if you agree to take a lesser draft pick for him."

Another solution could be that physicals for traded players are conducted by an independent, league-appointed doctor, rather than the team's medical staff. If that doctor says the player passes the physical, the team trading for the player can't back out.

The Jets and Chargers had different reactions to their trade partners asking to renegotiate the trades, but the situation both teams were faced with highlights potential problems that can arise with the NFL allowing teams to change the terms of trades after the trade deadline.

#2848. Patriots’ first 5 international games, revisited before Lions matchup

For the third time in the last four years, the New England Patriots have taken a trip abroad. On Sunday, the team of head coach Mike Vrabel will take on the Detroit Lions at the Allianz Arena in Munich, Germany.

It will be the club's second trip to Germany, and sixth international regular season game overall. In total, the Patriots are entering that contest with a 3-2 record, even though they are coming off two straight defeats outside the United States

With that said, let's take a closer look at the Patriots' international history.

2009 vs. Tampa Bay Buccaneers (London): The Patriots' first non-preseason game on international soil took place at London's Wembley Stadium during the 2009 season, and it was a blowout. Led by an opportunistic defense that had three interceptions — including a Brandon Meriweather pick-six — New England ended up beating Tampa Bay with a final score of 35-7.

2012 vs. St. Louis Rams (London): Three years after the Buccaneers were blown out by the Patriots at Wembley Stadium, the Rams suffered the same fate. While they did take a 7-0 lead, New England ended up scoring 45 unanswered after that. Tom Brady threw four touchdown passes en route to a 45-7 victory, with Rob Gronkowski as his favorite target: the two future Hall of Famers connected eight times for 146 yards and two scores.

2017 vs. Oakland Raiders (Mexico City): The Patriots' first trip to Mexico in two decades — more on that in a second — saw them leave the Raiders no chance. Tom Brady threw three touchdowns this time around, while Stephen Gostkowski went 4-for-4 on field goal tries including a career-long from 62 yards out. When all was said and done, New England had scored 33 points to the Raiders' 8, improving to 3-0 in international regular season games.

2023 vs. Indianapolis Colts (Frankfurt): The Patriots' first game in Germany, an international marketing territory for them, was a debacle from start to finish. Cornerback J.C. Jackson was scratched from the trip, questions about Bill Belichick's future started to get louder, and the contest itself was a low point even by the Patriots' 2023 standards. Their 10-6 defeat was topped off by quarterbacks Mac Jones and Bailey Zappe both throwing head-scratching interceptions on back-to-back fourth-quarter drives to seal the defeat.

2024 vs. Jacksonville Jaguars (London): Their return to the United Kingdom for the first time in 12 years ended in a second straight international defeat for the Patriots. Drake Maye's second career start saw him complete 70.3% of his passes for 276 yards and a pair of touchdowns, but his efforts were not enough to steady an uneven offense or overcome some shaky punt coverage and third down defense. When all was said and done, New England was beaten 32-16 to drop to 3-2 outside the United States.

While those five games are the most notable, the Patriots' international history is actually longer than that.

In addition to those games, they also played three preseason contests plus an interleague game abroad over the course of the years. The first of those came in August 1969, when the then-Boston Patriots met the Lions at Jarry Park in Montreal in an AFL-NFL clash. The game ended 22-9 in the Lions' favor.

The Patriots also came up empty in their next trips to Canada. Facing the Pittsburgh Steelers at Montreal's Olympic Stadium in the 1990 preseason, New England lost 30-14; three years after that, they were beaten 12-9 by the Cleveland Browns in Toronto. In 1998, they celebrated a 21-3 victory over the Dallas Cowboys in an exhibition showdown in Mexico City, as mentioned above.

The team also was scheduled to take on the Seattle Seahawks in the so-called China Bowl in August 2007. That game, which would have taken place in Beijing, was initially postponed to 2009 before being canceled altogether.

#2841. NFL Picks Against the Spread: Week 10 Games

Before the tenth Sunday of the 2026 regular season kicks off, let's have some fun by taking a look at the lines for the NFL Week 10 games.

You can CLICK HERE for straight up NFL picks without regard to the spread.

My overall record this season is 62-71-2, which is slightly ahead of the collective BGN community at 60-73-2. We're battling to get back to .500.

Here are suggestions when trying to beat the odds.

NEW ENGLAND PATRIOTS at DETROIT LIONS (-2.5): The Lions' offense has been struggling lately but Green Bay destroyed this Pats defense last week and Detroit could wake up here in this Germany game. PICK: Lions -2.5

MINNESOTA VIKINGS at GREEN BAY PACKERS (-2.5): Is Kirk Cousins going to be sharp in his first game back with the Vikings? Seems to me like there could be an adjustment period. And it's hard to bet against Jordan Love right now, even while going up against Brian Flores. The Packers aren't getting swept by Minny this season. PICK: Packers -2.5

HOUSTON TEXANS (-4.5) at CLEVELAND BROWNS: Feels like a meeting between two teams trending in opposite directions. The Texans are heating up after a slow start. The Browns are cooling off after a fast start. PICK: Texans -4.5

KANSAS CITY CHIEFS (-3.5) at ATLANTA FALCONS: This is a line I'd stay away from because the Falcons are a weird team to figure out on a weekly basis. But since I'm making a pick, I'll lean with Patrick Mahomes' side. PICK: Chiefs -3.5

MIAMI DOLPHINS at INDIANAPOLIS COLTS (-3.5): This is admittedly weird reasoning. But there's just something about how bizarrely the Eagles have handled their backup quarterback situation this year that makes me believe former Philly draft pick Kyle McCord is going to light it up for the Dolphins in this game. That feels like the next logical step in this progression. PICK: Dolphins +3.5

CAROLINA PANTHERS at NEW ORLEANS SAINTS (-1.5): The Panthers are riding a three-game losing streak, two of which were decided by more than one possession. This team isn't any good. Neither are the Saints … but at least they have a home crowd going for them. PICK: Saints -1.5

JACKSONVILLE JAGUARS (-10.5) at TENNESSEE TITANS: Would be pretty funny if the Jags followed up making big trade deadline acquisitions by losing to the lowly Titans … while resting their new additions, including Jordan Davis. But the Titans' outlook is pretty bleak. Can't take them. PICK: Jaguars -10.5

BUFFALO BILLS (-7.5) at NEW YORK JETS: Probably going to regret putting faith in Geno Smith but the Bills are coming off short rest and it's a division game. The Jets could conceivably cover in a loss. PICK: Jets +7.5

LOS ANGELES RAMS (-9.5) at ARIZONA CARDINALS: The Rams previously lost to the Cardinals. They're going to get their get-back here against rookie Carson Beck making his first NFL start. PICK: Rams -9.5

SEATTLE SEAHAWKS (-6.5) at LAS VEGAS RAIDERS: Fernando Mendoza making his first career start against Seattle's defense is a tough spot for the rook. PICK: Seahawks -6.5

SAN FRANCISCO 49ers (-4.5) at DALLAS COWBOYS: The 49ers absolutely own the Cowboys. No reason to think things will go differently for Dallas this time around. Brock Purdy has been balling; he should be able to carve up an entirely gettable Cowboys defense. PICK: 49ers -4.5

PITTSBURGH STEELERS at CINCINNATI BENGALS (-3.5): Joe Burrow is cooking right now. That said, the Steelers are not likely to go down without a fight. Pittsburgh, coming off their bye week, covers in a loss. PICK: Steelers +3.5

LOS ANGELES CHARGERS at BALTIMORE RAVENS (-4.5): Am I a sucker for believing the Bolts are going to win a game eventually!? Probably. PICK: Chargers +4.5

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2829. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2816. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2815. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2813. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations (PDF)

#2811. 3 things the Patriots have to do to beat the Lions

The New England Patriots are taking Sunday's game against the Detroit Lions quite seriously. Not only do they want to wash the taste of last week's 45-27 defeat versus Green Bay from their collective mouths, they also hope to enter the upcoming bye week with a 6-4 record and some much-needed momentum on their side.

Doing so will not be easy; the Lions are a formidable opponent making the trip to Germany off a four-game winning streak. However, there are ways for the Patriots to defeat them and it starts with the following crucial areas.

Jahmyr Gibbs is a problem. Just ask any team that has had to face him this season.

Appearing in all eight of the Detroit Lions' games, the versatile running back has gained more than 100 scrimmage yards six times. On the year, he is ranked first in the NFL with 1,132 of them and also has found the end zone a league-leading 11 times.

He is the motor that powers an offense that has been among the most productive in football thus far in 2026. And, unsurprisingly, he is a player the New England Patriots are making sure to keep a close eye on this Sunday.

"Gibbs is probably the best running back in the league right now as far as his target share, the amount of yards, in terms of how much offense he is producing by himself," said safety Kevin Byard. "He's the No. 1 target that we have to stop, for sure."

Joining the Lions as a first-round draft pick in 2023, Gibbs' career is off to a red-hot start. He is ranked first in the NFL in touchdowns since starting his career (60) and second in scrimmage yards (6,161). He also was voted to the Pro Bowl in every season thus far.

"Probably one of the best players in the NFL," said defensive coordinator Zak Kuhr at the FC Bayern Munich practice facility earlier this week. "He's got the most touches, all-purpose yards, forced missed tackles, explosive runs. He's a threat out of the backfield."

While the Lions offense as a whole is filled to the brim with talent — including quarterback Jared Goff, who isn't going to be underestimated by the Patriots — Gibbs is the main man. The 24-year-old has touched the ball on over 40% of Detroit's plays and is responsible for 37% of the team's yardage output, despite only averaging 5.4 yards per touch. He also has scored 11 of 25 Detroit touchdowns this season.

So, how do the Patriots actually stop him? Maybe by taking a page out of the Dolphins' playbook. Miami didn't do anything fancy with blitzes or stunts — they just built a wall, filled lanes quickly, and mostly swarmed well to keep Gibbs from getting more yards even when he broke tackles. He finished that game with a season-low 74 yards and a touchdown.

They may not be the Packers, who had averaged 4.7 explosive pass plays per game entering their contest against the Patriots last week (before adding seven more to their tally), but the Lions are more than capable of putting pressure on New England's pass defense as well. Another performance like last Sunday's would therefore be a problem for the team.

So, what do you do if you're the Patriots? There are multiple ways to tackle this problem beyond just better execution from the players on the field, and they all start at the very most important spot: quarterback.

Jared Goff may not be one of the NFL's top-tier quarterbacks, but he has been pretty darn good getting the football to his weapons. That group is highlighted by the aforementioned Jahmyr Gibbs – just look at the numbers – but also includes other talented players in wide receivers Amon-Ra St. Brown and Jameson Williams as well as tight end Sam LaPorta. All of them are dangerous with the ball in their hands, so preventing it from getting there looks like a natural first step to success.

In order to accomplish that particular goal against a player dubbed an "elite pocket passer" by the team this week, one position group in particular will be in the spotlight: the interior defensive line, and most prominently the top trio of Milton Williams, Christian Barmore and Cory Durden. They know it, too.

"He's one of the best in the league. If they keep him clean, he has a clean pocket, he can make any throw," Williams explained. "They have a lot of weapons over there. Good offensive line. If he can play with a clean pocket and time to throw, he can make any throw with any quarterback in this league."

Whether or not the Patriots can bring pressure remains to be seen, but the Lions have been good at keeping Jared Goff clean so far this season. He has been pressured on only 31.8% of his dropbacks, according to Pro Football Focus, the seventh-lowest number in the league.

It might be time for the Patriots' interior duo of Williams and Barmore to live up to their quite sizable paychecks.

There is one universal truth when it comes to passing the football: it's hard to do so when your quarterback is on his butt. Drake Maye, to his credit, hasn't had to experience this quite as much as he had to earlier in his career. That said, while he is still pressured at the fourth-highest rate in the NFL this season (43.3%) and ranked eighth in most sacks taken (20).

Those numbers seeing an increase on Sunday could very well happen. The Lions, after all, have shown they can get after the quarterback. Led by edge rusher Aidan Hutchinson and interior lineman Alim McNeill, they are ranked third in the NFL with 27 sacks – 10.5 of those coming from the standout duo.

The Patriots' problems do not end with the talent Detroit has at its disposal. The team's own personnel is also a question mark. On the one hand, you have an offensive line that will start yet another different combination along its interior with Ben Brown out at left guard. Greg Van Roten, who started one game in lieu of Mike Onwenu at right guard earlier in the year, and Walter Rouse, who only participated twice on a limited basis in practice this year, are the candidates to replace him, as are current right-side guard Matt Pryor and practice squad elevatee Andrew Rupcich.

In addition, the status of running back Rhamondre Stevenson is also unclear. The sixth-year man suffered a knee injury last Sunday versus Green Bay and like Rouse only practiced twice as a limited participant this week. There are positive signs about his outlook – the Patriots did not elevate a running back to their game day roster – but how involved he will be especially in pass protection has yet to be seen.

At the end of the day, the Patriots will need to find a way to get the job done regardless of the available personnel. Because if they do, their chances of moving the ball through the air do look good against a defense that has been vulnerable at times at the second and third levels.

#2809. Film study: What is the Wizards’ offense missing?

Before Thursday night's barnburner of a loss to the Pistons in double overtime, the vibes around the Wizards, and their offense in particular, were nearing a free fall. The Wiz have the league's 26th-ranked offense, even after the onslaught against Detroit.

There were, to be sure, positive signals to glean. Alex Sarr played by far his best game of the season, knocking down four threes. Trae Young showed more scoring juice, shooting 17 free throws and hitting a buzzer-beater to force OT. Sarr and Anthony Davis connected on a rare lob in overtime.

But the underlying issues behind the bricktastic 4-9 start will take longer to solve. The Wizards barely shoot any threes, and miss most of the ones they do try. Only the Bulls have shot a worse percentage from deep so far. With poor spacing and clumsy jumbo lineups, the offense has tilted concerningly toward true Trae Young heliocentrism; Young leads the league in seconds per touch.

The Wizards use core actions that make a fair amount of sense for their personnel. They rely on side-to-side movement, hoping to launch Young downhill and create better angles for the pick and roll. Consider this frequent action, with Kyshawn George maneuvering to the wing (pending the much-awaited return of NBA.com's video clips, I'm using screenshots for now):

That's an Iverson cut, intended to distract the defense and free up a Young-AD pick and roll. When Young starts going downhill, Tre Johnson flies up in tandem from the opposite corner. But the Pacers easily box Young toward the wing and funnel the ball away from the middle:

Young makes the pass to AJ Dybantsa, but Pascal Siakam closes him down, and Dybantsa, with no advantage, throws the ball away. This is not an uncommon sight for the Wizards — a Young pick and roll in tight confines near the sideline, putting minimal pressure on the opposing center.

The Wizards have to find a way to make plays like this impact the defense more. They're simply not getting enough out of their secondary actions. Above, for example, Johnson sprinting up from the corner is a good idea in theory. But nobody is screening for Johnson here. What if AD had turned at speed and set a pindown for him, just as the defense expects a screen for Young?

The best offenses pivot into second and third actions. These are built-in counters, depending on how the defense guards the initial one. Consider Sarr in this position, after running a pick and pop:

Bilal Coulibaly's cut through the paint is a scripted thing many teams with shooting bigs do. It's meant to drag away the help defender closest to Sarr — the guy whose job it would be to stunt toward Sarr and contest a three-pointer. But here, Sarr set the screen near half-court and barely made contact. Larry Nance Jr. recovered easily; no rotation needed.

This first action didn't yield much, which is normal. The next step for the Wiz is moving quickly and effectively into something else, and making the right reads from there. This is where the above play ended up:

Sarr dribbles into a hand-off with Johnson, who has sprinted into the space that Coulibaly vacated. This looks promising; George is spaced one pass away, and Johnson has his defender on his back. But Johnson doesn't see the pass to George in time, and Jalen Slawson blows up his dribble for a dunk the other way.

They have the right idea here. They're trying to use Sarr and Johnson in their optimal roles. But defenses see these kinds of actions every game. Without a higher level of precision — physical, well-timed screens, and snappy passing — these plays look ordinary and even aimless.

There are ways to loosen this up. Step one, of course, is to make some shots. (Even Thursday was a mixed bag in this regard, with Johnson shooting 1-of-9 from three.) Getting more consistent offense from wings other than George, who is leveling up before our eyes, would be a godsend. Most of all, the Wizards need Young to find more success probing around and behind bigs, rather than ending up in these situations:

The Wizards have been focused on running these "angle" pick and rolls near the sideline. They are useful for generating pocket passes to the bigs, while spacing guys on the other side. But too often, with Young struggling to separate off the dribble, opposing teams are using aggressive coverages and pinning Young to the sideline.

Tough games are looming against feisty, athletic Western Conference teams in Houston and Memphis. With a 4-9 record, they'll need to find a way to create more advantages in short order.

Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent, and his counselor David Zervos, are among the names being floated to replace Hassett, according to Semafor, which cited unnamed people familiar with his plans.

#2803. Kings head coach Doug Christie gets brutally honest about job security

SACRAMENTO ― The Sacramento Kings have dropped another game and if you think head coach Doug Christie is worried about being in some kind of hot seat, he's not. And he shouldn't be.

It's a familiar position for Christie, who's been dealt a bad hand since his head coaching tenure began. He inherited a then-Mike Brown team that was a season removed from the postseason before eventually trading its franchise player, De'Aaron Fox. They finished 40-42, Christie went 27-24 as interim, having taken over after a 13-18 start.

Last season, as an official first-year coach, without the interim tag, multiple players in his projected lineup, including Domantas Sabonis and Keegan Murray, missed large portions of the season, and the Kings finished with its worst record in franchise history since moving to Sacramento at 22-60.

And now, the Kings are having one of the worst starts in franchise history, dropping to 1-11 after their Friday night loss to the Portland Trail Blazers, 120-107, on Nov. 13 during the NBA Cup. It's the worst start since the 1990-91 season, in which that team started 1-13.

"I think this seat is hot always, so it doesn't really matter," Christie told USA TODAY about potentially being in the hot seat. "Obviously, we want to win games. Nobody wants to win more than me, from the standpoint that the record doesn't go on the player's numbers. The record goes on coach's number."

When Scott Perry became Kings general manager in April 2025, one of his first moves was to sign Christie to a three-year deal, worth about $2 million per year with a team option in the final year, 2027-28. Christie remained leading the charge after coaching to the worst franchise record in team history since 2008-2009 (17-65), as Perry attributed that tumultuous season to injuries, according to The Athletic.

It's much of the same this season, with a season-ending injury to Ben Simmons and second-year center Maxime Raynaud missing the start of the season with a knee injury.

"We come in, continue to teach, continue to push," Christie said. "As we get all these guys back and get them healthy, and then we continue down the path of trying to put together what we thought we would be putting together when we started this process."

The Kings are reportedly hesitant to part ways with Christie, according to The Athletic's Sam Amick. Former Sacramento Kings legend and current Sacramento State head coach Mike Bibby sat front row to get a witness of the the beatdown handed to the Kings.

The former guard, who played in Sacramento from 2001-2008, showed support for his former teammate, saying he wasn't the one to blame during a postgame appearance with Morgan Ragan and Deuce Mason on NBC Sports California.

"I don't blame Doug (Christie)," Bibby said. "I don't think Doug is the problem. Doug's been there for a long time, I think Doug could help, but getting rid of Doug's not the problem."

However, Bibby did say it was "time for some changes". Although he wasn't sure where those changes needed to come from, he advised the team to trade guys like Domantas Sabonis, Zach LaVine and Malik Monk to put them in better positions while the Kings start over. It happened to Bibby in his day when the Kings were no longer winning and they traded him to the Atlanta Hawks in 2008.

"When I was here I didn't wanna leave, but it was time to go," Bibby said. "For those guys put them in good situations like Sabonis, LaVine, put them in situations that could help them and get some good stuff back. It's going to take a couple of years to get the Kings going again."

Kings: Darius Acuff Jr. is making history, but is losing taking a toll?

If there's one thing Christie knows, it's how hard the Sacramento fans go for the Kings. He's experienced it as a player, and as both assistant and head coach he's seen how they've stuck beside the team through multiple years of losing seasons and the breakthrough when they finally cracked the postseason after a 16-year drought.

Christie's message to the fans: "it won't always be like this."

"This is a process that we're going through to try to get to where we want to go, and their loyalty means everything," Christie told USA TODAY. " When I see them around town, they're always (saying) 'coach keep going, keep pushing,' and that's what I do every day. I'll always be the first one in the building, working until we figure this thing out."

Kings forward Keegan Murray had a similar message to the fans to keep supporting, despite the shortcomings.

"I love our fans. They've been great to us... to me. I appreciate them coming out to support, we know that Rome's not built in a day but at the end of the day we're trying," Murray told USA TODAY. "With their support, it'll only get better."

Murray, Sabonis and Monk are the few Kings players that have been around for their lone playoff appearance in the last 15 years. Murray often tells the team, especially Acuff who was down in the dumps after a recent home loss, about how loud Golden 1 Center can get when their team is rolling.

"When we're really good, this place is better than any arena in the NBA," Murray said. "I feel like that's a big motivating factor in what we're trying to get to. ... I'm just hoping we can get back on track."
