#2729. Is everyone on Nets roster available for trade? Mostly yes

Is "everyone" on the Brooklyn Nets roster available for the right price as Sam Amick reported Friday in a round-up of trade rumors?!? Even 20-year-old Mikel Brown Jr. and Egor Demin, the organization's two highest draft picks over the past 30 years?!? Seems a bit much, but with the Nets record disappointing as they get ready to play the Memphis Grizzlies at the end of a four-game road trip, the general sentiment appears true, as the The Athletic reporter noted.

If you call the Brooklyn Nets (3-8) about any of their players, they will listen. Per a team source, it's just that simple.

Such is life when you're a rebuilding team, not to mention an organization that has two veterans — Michael Porter Jr. and Julius Randle — who could help any number of contenders.

Those two players are widely discussed as top targets in league circles, with Porter (21.5 points and 7.5 rebounds per) above all the rest of the Nets. The 28-year-old small forward who won a title with Denver in 2023 is in the last year of his deal ($40.8 million). Randle, the 31-year-old forward who helped Minnesota reach the West finals in 2025, has a player option worth $35 million for next season.

Two days ago, Jake Fischer of The Steinline offered a similar assessment.

An expectation persists in some corners of the league that Brooklyn, which owes its first-rounder in June to Houston as a lingering vestige of the acquisition of James Harden in January 2021, might indeed decide to shop veterans such as Michael Porter Jr. via trade between now and Feb. 11 to see if it can capitalize on the anticipated lack of true sellers in the marketplace.

So, it's increasingly a given in NBA circles that the Nets will be a bazaar come February 12 at 3:00 p.m. EST or maybe even before, say December 15, the unofficial start of trade season when the dozens of players signed or re-signed over the Summer become available for the first time. The larger the pool of players, the more possibilities that GMs and capologists can noodle. Of course with all the injuries across the league, it's possible teams will want to find replacements earlier than that.

MPJ is the MMP of the group == Most Mentioned Player — for a number of reasons. He is having yet another good season as Amick notes and is on an expiring deal. If the Nets don't want to extend him — they can up till June 30 — then they'd better do something or lose him without a return. That is not going to happen. Randle is in a bit of a similar situation but he isn't expiring. He's got a player option and while he will be four years older than Porter Jr. by the deadline, his contract load is lighter. MPJ is owed $40.6 million this season, compared to the $33.3 million Randle makes now and the $35.8 million he'll earn if he doesn't exercise his option. He is expected to.

Otherwise, what's the Nets rush? That's where it gets interesting. For one thing, the Draft and off-season are when Sean Marks & co. makes their biggest moves and it's also when teams examine their futures a lot more closely. They know what the need. It wouldn't be surprising if the Nets look to add more assets for a big trade, even if they already had 13 first rounders and 19 seconds available. Do they have their eye on someone? Almost certainly, they have a list, but as Marks admitted on Brooklyn's Media Day in September, there's a lot that has to be considered, starting with who other teams are willing to part with.

Marks was specifically asked at Media Day when he might "push all the chips to the middle of the table" and make the big move for that player, as he has said, who will "absolutely and automatically change the trajectory of the franchise." He was generally non-committal, but did identify the factors that will come into play.

"We've got a couple more rookies this year to add into the fray as well," Marks said, suggesting he wants to see how his young players do this season. "But then it's also a combination of what becomes available, right? I can't predict when players from other teams become available, what free agency looks like down the road and so forth. So, I think that's where you just keep your flexibility and you know, like, okay, when's the right time to go for this?

"We're always listening. I'm not sure we're going to be any more active than we have been in the past, but that doesn't mean we're not listening. That doesn't mean we're not shaking trees and seeing what's out there. I think a lot will be predicated first and foremost on what this roster looks like, and we'll get a good feel for that pretty early." (Emphasis added.)

Of course, the Nets has been very very active in the recent past with the two franchise-defining trades with the Knicks and Rockets just before the the Draft in 2024, or the multiple salary dump deals just before the Draft in 2025 that got them MPJ and the Nuggets 2032 pick and the Hawks No. 22 pick. or just before the Draft in 2026 that got them Randle in the T'Wolves first round pick.

Bottom line, we think, is that the Nets are holding their cards close, maybe even closer than usual (which is saying something.) It makes sense, of course, because you can't keep accumulating assets forever. You have to move.

#2727. Domantas Sabonis Trade Rumors: Pros & Cons For Charlotte

The Sacramento Kings have stumbled out to a dismal 1–11 start and are shifting their franchise timeline around electric rookie guard Darius Acuff Jr. As a result, trade speculation around Domantas Sabonis, the Kings' three-time All-Star center, has heated up.

According to reports from Sam Amick, Sacramento is open to moving virtually anyone on its roster except Acuff Jr., with the Charlotte Hornets and Toronto Raptors emerging among the primary expected suitors ahead of the February 11 trade deadline. On paper, adding a perennial double-double machine to a 4–8 Hornets roster sounds tempting—but when you weigh the basketball fit, financial cost, and General Manager Jeff Peterson's long-term blueprint, pursuing Sabonis carries as many structural concerns as rewards.

Nov 10, 2026; Sacramento, California, USA; Sacramento Kings forward Domantas Sabonis (11) reacts after a play during the fourth quarter against the Orlando Magic at Golden 1 Center. Mandatory Credit: Sergio Estrada-Imagn Images

There is no mystery about what Sabonis would fix in Charlotte. Through 12 games, the Hornets have struggled to replace LaMelo Ball's offense. They've struggled mightily to generate clean scoring flow, ranking at the bottom of the league in points and coughing up possessions at a high rate. Dropping Sabonis into Head Coach Charles Lee's system alters that.

Even in a quieter scoring year in Sacramento (14.5 points per game), Sabonis sits near the very top of the league in rebounding at 13.2 boards a night while dishing out 5.8 assists per contest on nearly 65 percent shooting from the floor. He would step in as the primary offensive hub, operating out of dribble-handoff actions that create open catch-and-shoot looks for his teammates without forcing them to manufacture every look entirely off the bounce.

The drawbacks become clear as soon as you examine how Sabonis fits into Charlotte's defensive identity. While Sabonis finishes possessions well on the defensive glass, he offers minimal vertical rim protection or switchability on the back line. Pairing Sabonis next to Naz Reid in the starting frontcourt gives Charles Lee plenty of offensive skill, yet leaves Charlotte without a true shot-blocking anchor at the rim—forcing the rest of the lineup to cover for him in space rather than relying on back-line deterrence.

The financial and asset costs are even harder to ignore. Even though Charlotte holds the largest traded player exception in NBA history at $40.77 million—created in the LaMelo Ball deal—Sabonis' $45.5 million salary this season still exceeds that exception. Because CBA rules prohibit combining a trade exception with outgoing player salaries to absorb a single contract, Jeff Peterson couldn't use that historic tool to acquire Sabonis cleanly, forcing Charlotte to ship out multiple rotation contracts just to make the math work.

Worse yet, Sabonis is owed $48.6 million in 2027–28—right when young Hornets star Brandon Miller's five-year, $209 million extension kicks in at roughly $36 million per year alongside existing long-term deals. Committing nearly $94 million over two years to a 30-year-old center—while likely sacrificing young frontcourt depth or draft capital in the deal—would wipe out the future cap flexibility Peterson has carefully built

Beyond the Xs and Os, there is also the human element to consider. Sabonis welcomes a change of scenery because he wants to compete in a winning environment, and jumping from a 1–11 Kings squad to a rebuilding 4–8 Hornets team hardly offers the playoff push he is chasing—especially with an Eastern Conference suitor like Toronto also lurking. Unless Sacramento's asking price drops to expiring contracts and minimal draft capital, Charlotte is far better off sticking to its long-term plan and letting its young core develop organically rather than forcing a costly splash.

#2721. Pinheiro Braathen seals back-to-back World Cup victories with slalom win in Levi

LEVI, Finland (AP) — Two races, two wins.

The World Cup skiing season couldn't have started any better for Lucas Pinheiro Braathen.

The Brazilian skier who earned South America its first medal at the Winter Olympics won the opening World Cup slalom in Levi, Finland, by 0.56 seconds on Sunday.

It follows up on his victory in the giant slalom in Sölden last month, which was the only other World Cup race so far this season.

The Norwegian-born Pinheiro Braathen led by 0.65 from Clement Noel after the first run and stayed ahead of the Frenchman, who placed second. Olympic slalom champion Loic Meillard was a further 0.17 back in third.

Pinheiro Braathen, who won the race in Levi last year too, now has 10 career World Cup wins — including five since he started representing Brazil after switching nationalities two years ago to represent his mother's home country.

To recap, here are ways we recommend watching NFL games in 2026.

YouTube TV gets you nearly every channel you need to watch the NFL, including Fox, NBC, ABC, CBS, ESPN and NFL Network. Plus, YouTube TV is the home of NFL Sunday Ticket. The Sunday Ticket package, while pricey on its own, gets you all the Sunday out-of-market games (more than 160 games per season).

Current pricing for new users of the 2026 Sunday Ticket is $27 per month for four months. Factor in the cost of YouTube TV's Sports and News plan, currently available at $49.99/month for your first 5 months, then $71.99/month thereafter.

That's $76.99 a month for the next four months of YouTube TV + NFL Sunday Ticket. You can also opt, with or without a YouTube TV subscription, to pay for the rest of the season of NFL Sunday Ticket upfront for just $108.

DirecTV's Choice tier (currently $87.98/month after fees for your first month, $107.98/month after fees for months two and three) gives you access to nearly every channel you need to watch NFL games year-round: NFL Network, ESPN, NBC, CBS, Fox, SEC Network and an abundance of regional sports networks. DirecTV Choice (and most other DirecTV plans) also comes with free ESPN Unlimited, which gets you access to every ESPN channel, an amazing value and a must for watching tons of college and pro sports all year round.

An ad-supported Peacock subscription ($12.99/month) lets you stream live sports and events airing on NBC, including Sunday Night Football and the rest of the NFL season. Plus, you'll get access to thousands of hours of shows and movies, including beloved sitcoms such as Parks and Recreationand The Office, every Bravo show and much more.

For $19.99/month, you can upgrade to an ad-free subscription, which includes live access to your local NBC affiliate (not just during designated sports and events) and the ability to download select titles to watch offline.

You can also get an ad-supported subscription for free with a Walmart+ membership, which is now included with a Sam's Club Plus membership. Sam's Club Plus costs $120 a year and includes members-only pricing on certain Sam's Club products, free delivery, a membership to Walmart+ (valued at $98 a year), and a subscription to either Paramount+ or Peacock ($90-130 a year). That's a lot of savings and perks rolled into one subscription.

Fubo's Sports package gives you access to ESPN, NFL Network, Fox, ABC, CBS, NBC and 20-plus more live channels. Starting at $54.99 for your first month, it offers most of the channels you'll need to watch the NFL season.

#2712. Practical, possible, and unlikely trades for Jalen Green

The Phoenix Suns are starting to work things out.

Winners of five of their last six, after a 1-5 start, the team is now 5-6 and recently beat the streaking Houston Rockets after outplaying them down the stretch. However, questions remain about starting guard Jalen Green's fit as he continues to average career lows as a scorer and from three, and the team is lacking a starting-level point guard alongside Devin Booker.

Questions have further arisen about his future in Phoenix after NBA Insider Chris Haynes reported Friday that the Suns have interest in acquiring point guard Dejounte Murray from the Pelicans, who recently requested a trade. With the two making similar amounts, the teams could trade them for each other without including any additional salary. But with heightened expectations after a surprising 2025-2026 campaign, the Suns could be looking to deal Green to more than just New Orleans to help their point guard problem. His $36 million salary gives the franchise some flexibility if they can find a good trade partner.

A common trade structure, the aforementioned deal is a classic "my problem for your problem" type of deal. Similar to the Cleveland Cavaliers and Los Angeles Clippers swap of Darius Garland and James Harden back in February, Green and Murray fit each other's situation better than their current ones.

Now 30 years old and coming off a torn Achilles, Murray better fits a team looking to compete for the playoffs this season, and since Joe Dumars and Troy Weaver took over leadership in New Orleans, the Pelicans have been acquiring young players such as Jermiah Fears and Derik Queen to build for the future.

With rumors continuing to heat up about both guards' futures, this seems like the most likely and easiest deal the Suns could pull off.

Bucks Receive: Jalen Green, 2027 First-Round pick (least favorable of Cleveland, Minnesota, and Utah)

While Herro would create the same problem Green does from a point-guard perspective, he's a significant upgrade and would give the Suns a more efficient, reliable scorer. With the Bucks already out most of their future picks because of their failed efforts to build a competitive roster for Giannis Antetokounmpo during his final years in Milwaukee, and potentially facing punishment for the contract they gave Gary Trent Jr., the Bucks could be looking for every opportunity to add future draft compensation.

In the last year of his four-year extension he signed back in 2022 when he was with the Heat, the Suns would likely need some assurance that Herro would be willing to stay in Phoenix before making the deal, but he would be a major talent upgrade over Green and had the same college coach as Devin Booker in John Calipari.

Trail Blazers Receive: Jalen Green, 2027 First-Round pick (least favorable of Cleveland, Minnesota, and Utah)

The Portland Trail Blazers already have too many guards, and adding another inefficient, G League Ignite alum guard alongside Scoot Henderson would not make sense for one of the league's biggest surprises through the first four weeks of the season, even if it got them a first-round pick; acquiring Holiday would make a world of difference for the Suns.

Playing alongside Booker in multiple Olympics and facing off against him in the 2021 NBA Finals, Holiday is a proven veteran playing in a bench role because of the Blazers' guard-heavy roster. He doesn't need the ball to score and knows how to win at the highest level. He's the only NBA player this decade with multiple Olympic Gold Medals and Finals victories. He's made the playoffs six straight seasons and helped both the Bucks and Celtics break their 15+ year title droughts. Many consider him to be the piece that made Boston's 2023-2024 team such a dominant one.

Who else should the Suns consider for Green?

#2711. Is Kentucky’s Mark Pope Finished?

Kentucky's Mark Pope has had a tough row to hoe lately. Last season, the fans were grumbling about their coach. This year already, Kentucky took a 20-point beating from Kansas in the Champions Classic, and were nearly upset in Rupp Arena by Northern Arizona.

It's hard to overstate how shocking this is. NAU is in Flagstaff. If Tucson is fairly remote, relatively speaking (which it is), Flagstaff is even more so.

The Lumberjacks have never been much of a factor in college basketball. It's only about 2.5 hours from Phoenix, but it's up in the mountains, there's not a lot going on there, and it just lives in a different orbit.

It's even a step below Grand Canyon University when it comes to basketball (Flagstaff is much closer to the magnificent canyon than is the university, which is actually in Phoenix).

In other words, at least when it comes to basketball, the Lumberjacks shouldn't even be mentioned in the same conversation as Kentucky.

Yet there they were, just a few seconds from one of the most shocking upsets anyone could have imagined.

Only a last-second three-point shot by Alex Wilkins kept Kentucky from a devastating loss.

To make things worse, this is what Pope said after the game: "Well, I hope everybody in Rupp had fun. Like it was a pretty exciting game … It came down to the last second. It's pretty entertaining if not well executed. It's really entertaining. I think this will be a fun team to follow. Like I said, this team. With one exception, they're finding ways to win games. And we'll continue that … as we try to settle the ship and execute better."

As a Kentucky grad himself, he should know better. Big Blue Nation expects to compete at the highest level, and to win, too. That response will not go over well.

And in fact, towards the end of the game, chants of "Fire Mark Pope" rang down from the stands.

It's his third season in Lexington, and the impatience is obvious. Unfortunately for the university, his buyout is around $17 million until April 1, when it falls to around $13 million.

At this point, assuming that Kentucky continues on its current trajectory and that the university can stomach the buyout, it might be time to consider who UK pursues as its next coach. Last time, you'll recall, Kentucky's big-game hunt didn't go too well. Dan Hurley was the focus, and they were never going to get him.

First, it would be a step down. What can he do at Kentucky that he hasn't done at UConn? And second, wife Andrea has been pretty firm about staying in the Northeast.

So going after a big name coach might not work out too well. They'll have to send out feelers, though. Here are some people we'd expect to see on the list.

Todd Golden. He's done as well as anyone since taking the Florida job, but like Hurley, what can he do in Lexington that he can't do in Gainesville? There's also that inconvenient investigation that saw Golden face charges of stalking. It was investigated under Title IX, but at least one of the women who accused him was not a Florida student. The chances of an embarrassing civil suit are still there.

T.J. Otzelberger. He's a Midwest guy, but Kentucky is much bigger than Iowa State. He's fairly young, too.

Grant McCasland. He's won at every stop, and with the resources Kentucky has, he might be tempted to leave Texas Tech. You could do a lot worse.

Nate Oats. His name comes up in every major search, but he's said he's happy at Alabama, plus the murder case involving members of his team is something of a demerit.

Ben McCollum. It amazed us that D-1 schools didn't pursue him more actively when he was at D-II Northwest Missouri State. His career winning percentage is, according to Iowa's official site, "the fifth-best mark all-time among collegiate men's basketball coaches." However, he's now coaching in his hometown, and Iowa's site points out that he's signed through the 2032 season in the first sentence of his bio. He's a brilliant coach, but good luck getting him.

Mark Byington. We thought UNC should have pursued him more arduously, but, like UK, they went big-game hunting before settling on Michael Malone. He'd do well.

Jai Lucas. That's who we'd go after. He was on John Calipari's staff previously, and everyone understands his potential. He's young, he's energetic, and he was superb in his first year at Miami. If he has a big year this year, he'll absolutely be a target. Whether he wants the job or not is a different matter.

Kentucky made an arrogant assumption about the last search, assuming that the job was so impressive that all the big name coaches would come crawling. It didn't work out that way, of course. Coaching at Kentucky is a very difficult job, largely because of the fans. Your margin of error is very thin. The pressure is why a lot of coaches want no part of this job.

But that's not true of everyone. There are plenty of young coaches who would kill for that job, even knowing there's a chance it could break their career.

#2709. Skipper Bryce sets new Scotland run record in win over UAE

United Arab Emirates 138-4 (20 overs): Oza 64, Hotchandani 43; Gordon 2-24

Scotland 142-3 (18.1 overs): K Bryce 51, S Bryce 43; Dharnidharka 2-22

Captain Kathyrn Bryce became Scotland Women's all-time leading run scorer as she hit an unbeaten half century to Scotland to a seven-wicket win over host nation United Arab Emirates at the ICC Women's Emerging Nations Trophy.

The skipper hit seven fours on her way to 51 not out as Scotland reached 142-3 with 11 balls to spare.

It takes her international run total to 3562, one more than Kari Carswell.

Her sister, Sarah Bryce, made 43, batting at number three after Darcey Carter's departure in the first over.

Katherine Fraser added 15, with Pippa Sproul on 16 not out.

Batting first, captain Esha Oza led the way with 64 not out as UAE reached 138-4 in their 20 overs.

Heena Hotchandani added 43 before she was one of two batters to be stumped by wicketkeeper Sarah Bryce from the bowling of Kirstie Gordon.

Having previously beaten Papua New Guinea and lost to Sri Lanka, Scotland sit second in Group A and are assured of a place in the Super Six stage.

Scotland conclude their group games against Ireland on Wednesday.

#2707. Kyrie Irving was recognized in Cleveland, in honor of his Game 7 shot to secure the 2016 NBA Finals

Kyrie Irving got a moment in the spotlight during the first timeout of Saturday's game between the Dallas Mavericks (7-5) and the Cleveland Cavaliers (6-7) at Rocket Arena.

The home crowd roared in appreciation as Irving's step-back 3-pointer to seal the Cavaliers' Game 7 win over the Golden State Warriors in the 2016 NBA Finals was replayed on the video boards at midcourt.

Irving tapped his chest and thanked the crowd as the Mavs left their huddle to return to the floor with 7:32 left in the first quarter. He may not have left the Cavaliers on the best terms a season later, but that shot proved to be the clincher in the franchise's first and only NBA Championship. He asked for a trade in the 2017 offseason, and the Cavaliers sent him to the Boston Celtics in exchange for Isaiah Thomas, Jae Crowder, Ante Žižić, and a 2018 first-round draft pick.

The Cavs won Game 7, 93-89, scoring just 18 points in the fourth quarter that night. They held Stephen Curry and the Warriors to just 13 points down the stretch. Irving scored five, and someone named LeBron James had 11 as Cleveland claimed the title behind their own version of Batman and Robin.

"I wanted to prove everybody wrong, and I wanted to prove myself right," Irving said in an interview with NBA.com before the game. "What a time. Shoutout to all of my teammates that were part of that team. It was a hard-fought season. I was coming off injury, going through a lot of emotions throughout the year and just being uncertain whether I could get back to the level I believed I could get back to. After we won, I was still like, 'Is there more?' Now when I look at this [shot], there's just this appreciation of my youth, my fearlessness — things I attribute my success to today. Game 7, NBA Finals — that's the pinnacle of any player's career. "

He rose up over Curry, kept his right elbow tucked and sank one of the most historic shots in NBA Finals history as a 24-year-old that night, just five years into what has blossomed into a 15-year, Hall of Fame-worthy career. He scored 26 points and pulled down six rebounds that night, just two games after putting together a 41-point masterpiece in the Cavs' win in Game 5.

James, who scored a matching 41 points in Game 5 that year, would win NBA Finals MVP as he and Irving brought a title to his hometown team.

The Cleveland crowd also gave Irving an ovation when his name was called during the pregame introductions for the starting lineups.

#2704. Vikings Trying to Keep Kirk Cousins Beyond 2026

No quarterback in NFL history has played 18 regular-season games in one season. Kirk Cousins has a chance to be the first, and his second run with the Minnesota Vikings starts today at Lambeau Field, the same place he tore his Achilles in 2023.

The Vikings (6-2) have lost two straight, and Kyler Murray is ruled out with a hamstring injury. Kevin O'Connell named Cousins the starter on Friday, three days after the trade that brought him back from the Las Vegas Raiders for a 2027 third-round pick.

Head Coach Kevin O'Connell has announced that QB Kirk Cousins will start Sunday at Green Bay. pic.x.com/zSMqWo0pHQ

— Minnesota Vikings (@Vikings) November 13, 2026

Cousins made nine starts in Las Vegas before the deal, throwing for 2,340 yards, 20 touchdowns and eight interceptions with a 97.1 rating. Only Joe Burrow (23) has more touchdown passes this season.

Related: Who Lobbied for Kirk Cousins' Return to Minnesota and How It Happened

The Vikings have more than this season in mind for Cousins. Before the trade was done, they asked whether he'd be open to staying in Minnesota for 2027, according to Ian Rapoport (ESPN and NFL Network).

The Vikings brought in Kirk Cousins to help them this season, but they already are interested and have talked to the veteran quarterback about returning to Minnesota for the 2027 season, sources told NFL Network.

Before the Vikings completed their highly-anticipated blockbuster trade for Cousins on Tuesday, they had one more phone call to make. Team brass placed a call to agent Mike McCartney, who has represented Cousins for his entire career.

The message in that short call, Rapoport wrote, was that the Vikings want Cousins' return to Minnesota to be "more than a one-year rental."

McCartney told general manager Nolan Teasley and executive vice president of football operations Rob Brzezinski that he believed Cousins was more than open to it. With that, the trade with the Raiders was on, though there were "no firm offers or deadlines."

Some of the money is already on Minnesota's books. The Vikings took on the rest of Cousins' minimum salary for 2026, which counts $650,000 against this year's cap, plus a fully guaranteed $10 million roster bonus for 2027.

If Cousins is the starter next season, Rapoport wrote, that $10 million will be baked into a salary that is "sure to exceed that number." OverTheCap lists March 15 as the deadline to release or adjust the contract, and the rest of the deal is void years.

Cousins is set to be a free agent after this season, but if all goes well, don't be surprised if the sides find a way to stay together through 2027. The Vikings wanted to know if Cousins was open to talking about it — and he was … When [Cousins' agent] told general manager Nolan Teasley and executive vice president of football operations Rob Brzezinski that he believed Cousins was more than open to continuing the relationship into 2027 — with no firm offers or deadlines — the trade with the Las Vegas Raiders was on.

Related: Report: Vikings HC Probably Safe, Flores Likely Staying Too

Cousins may be set for free agency after this season but clearly both he and the Minnesota Vikings would like to have him in purple for one more year, while they try to figure out their future at the most enigmatic position in local sports history.

Teasley kept the focus on this season at his Nov. 11 press conference. "There is always potential for [beyond this season], but this [trade] was about 2026," he said. Cousins' first run in Minnesota lasted 88 games from 2018 to 2023, with 23,265 yards, 171 touchdowns and 55 interceptions. Today, that becomes 89.

#2703. Lazio, mercato e rinnovi: Cancellieri attende il parere di Gattuso

Per Cataldi, Lazzari e Pellegrini si prospetta una partenza a gennaio o la liberazione a giugno.

Cataldi, Lazzari e Pellegrini sono i tre giocatori con contratto in scadenza nel 2027 che la Lazio considera in uscita: potrebbero partire a gennaio oppure liberarsi a giugno. Secondo quanto riportato da Corriere dello Sport, Diversa la posizione del quarto, Cancellieri, per il quale resta possibile una trattativa. In attesa del verdetto della commissione di vigilanza sui conti, previsto a inizio dicembre, il prossimo mercato rischia un nuovo embargo nonostante il primo posto della squadra.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Secondo il Corriere dello Sport, Lotito e Fabiani stanno dando priorità ai rinnovi, distinguendo le posizioni in scadenza nel 2027 da quelle del 2028.

Danilo Cataldi ha 32 anni ed è tornato alla Lazio dal prestito alla Fiorentina nell’estate in cui il mercato biancoceleste era bloccato. Palladino aveva lasciato il club viola, ma ne avrebbe chiesto la conferma; dopo la sua partenza è arrivato Pioli. Cataldi è rimasto e, per Sarri, la sua presenza è stata importante in una stagione segnata dall’operazione di Rovella per la pubalgia e dal mancato impiego di Belahyane.

Anche Cataldi si è operato a fine stagione ed è rientrato soltanto pochi giorni fa. Sulla sua posizione peserebbero soprattutto gli attriti passati con Lotito. Una possibile destinazione è il Bologna, oggi allenato da Palladino: il trasferimento potrebbe avvenire a gennaio o a giugno, con una scelta che spetta al centrocampista.

Lazzari compirà 33 anni il 29 novembre e veste la maglia della Lazio dal 2019. Nell’agosto 2025 era stato vicino alla partenza: lo voleva il Sassuolo, ma la società biancoceleste, con il mercato bloccato, aveva rifiutato per non ridurre le alternative sulla destra. In quel ruolo si è affermato Floriani, mentre Marusic è sotto contratto fino al 2028, con un’opzione per il 2029.

Il terzo nome tra i possibili partenti è Luca Pellegrini. Tagliato a settembre, è poi rientrato su spinta di Gattuso: dopo gli addii di Patric e Romagnoli sarebbe rimasto l’unico fuori. Sulla sua situazione incidono l’arrivo di Pedraza e un ingaggio da 2,4 milioni. La quota fissa è di 1,9 milioni; i bonus che portano alla cifra superiore sono legati a presenze facilmente raggiungibili. Finora ne ha collezionata una in Coppa Italia.

Pellegrini valuterà le possibilità di mercato a gennaio. In alternativa, potrebbe liberarsi a giugno. Per Cancellieri lo scenario è differente: la società aveva trovato una soluzione per venderlo nelle ultime battute del mercato estivo, ma Gattuso ne ha fermato il trasferimento perché non voleva restare senza un’alternativa sulla destra.

Lo cercavano Parma e Fiorentina. Era stata definita un’operazione che avrebbe portato Gudmundsson alla Lazio e Cancellieri alla Fiorentina, con altri 5 milioni destinati a Lotito. La società ha poi comunque concluso l’arrivo di Gudmundsson in prestito. Tra i giocatori in scadenza nel 2027, Cancellieri è l’unico con cui potrebbe essere avviato un tentativo di rinnovo: sarà decisivo il parere di Gattuso.

#2700. 'This one's for him': McIlroy emotionally dedicates Race to Dubai win for ailing dad

Rory McIlroy was understandably emotional when talking about how much winning a record-tying eighth Race to Dubai title meant to "a proud European."

But it was a question about his father, Gerry, that choked up the 37-year-old.

DP World Tour reporter Tim Barter asked a victorious McIlroy, "You father is recovering from health issues over in America. How much was he in your thoughts today?"

"A lot," McIlroy responded, rubbing the side of his face, his voice becoming shaky. "Yeah, a lot."

"He's been in the hospital for the last two months. Underwent a huge operation at the end of September and he's recovering well, thankfully," McIlroy added.

"When I left to come up here to the Middle East, I went up to [the Cleveland Clinic] to see him before I went. And he said for me to go win the Race to Dubai. And uh ..."

With that, McIlroy tried his best to not break down and Barter helped him by saying how proud Gerry would be of his son, and ending the interview.

"Yeah," McIlroy said, "this one's for him."

#2699. Cavs beat Mavericks 127-117 behind Tyrese Proctor’s triple double

The Cleveland Cavaliers beat the Dallas Mavericks 127-117 behind an inspiring effort from their reserves.

The Cavs played on a skeleton crew tonight, with most of their usual rotation sidelined after last night's delay kept them in Miami longer than expected. Donovan Mitchell, James Harden, Peyton Watson, and Jarrett Allen all sat out while Evan Mobley, Sam Merrill, and Nae'Qwan Tomlin were out with respective injuries.

The starting lineup tonight was Tyrese Proctor, Meleek Thomas, Jaylon Tyson, Mario Hezonja, and Thomas Bryant.

The trio of Proctor, Thomas, and Tyson, who have labeled themselves as 'the pitbulls', set the tone early by being hyper-aggressive and active on both ends of the floor. They combined for 41 points in the first half, doing a little bit of everything to help build an 18-point lead going into the locker room.

Proctor finished the game with 24 points, 11 rebounds, and 10 assists. It was the strongest game of his career and proof of concept that Proctor is a foundational piece of this team's future.

Rocket Arena, which began the night feeling scorned by the stars who sat out, gave a hefty round of applause for the young group that showed up tonight.

Bryant and Hezonja deserve credit as well for serving as the veteran leaders of a younger, more inexperienced lineup. Hezonja scored 22 points on 9-of-12 shooting while Bryant added 19 points on 7-of-12 shooting.

Kyrie Irving scored 34 points and was given a warm reception both when he was announced in the Mavericks' starting lineup and after the Cavs played a video of his iconic Game 7 shot over the Warriors. It was nice to see Irving play basketball back in Cleveland.

The Cavs also saw support from two-way prospect Norchad Omier, who added 13 points and 8 rebounds by hustling around the court all night. It's performances like this that made a double-digit victory possible despite being short-handed.

Overall, the Cavs' youth showed that you can accomplish a lot by simply playing hard. The cohesion that was shown by this group tonight should be kept in mind moving forward.

Cleveland is back home on Monday against the Chicago Bulls.

#2695. The Knicks are struggling to finish games to start the season

It was just a few months ago that you could've turned a Knicks game off and celebrated within the last five minutes if it was a one-possession game: that's how automatic they were in the clutch.

Led by the former Clutch Player of the Year and an impeccable supporting cast, it seemed like every time they found themselves in a late and close situation, it was automatic after those two CJ McCollum games. Even going back further than that, this team's ability to dig deep when they need it most has been the defining characteristic of the Jalen Brunson era.

With an identical roster back in 2026-27, you'd expect it'd be the same… except it's not.

In the Knicks' 6-6 start, they've played seven clutch games. They've lost six of them.

Not all clutch games are created equal, but all of these games were winnable. Even last night's loss against Indiana, which I was saying felt like a "fake clutch game," as the Knicks got a few buckets late to make it look close, they somehow managed to have the ball with a chance to send it to overtime at the buzzer.

One way or another, the Knicks have failed to win these games. The lone win, the high-scoring thriller in Chicago, was the only game where it's felt like last season's Knicks that found a way.

Overall, in their 1-6 record in clutch games, the Knicks have a minus-17.4 net rating, ranking 22nd in the NBA. They don't do anything truly bad in these games, but they do nothing well.

Their turnover rate is league average, as is their assist rate. Their rebounding goes from dominant to so-so, but the real culprit is the putrid 50.7 TS%, which ranks 23rd. That's the catalyst behind an offense that hasn't worked in these situations.

If you exclude the Chicago game, they have a minus-40 net rating with an 82.9 offensive rating and an even rougher 44.4 TS%. All six of those games have come against the type of teams they've excelled against in postseason settings, but it just hasn't happened so far.

Going game-by-game, it's just a mess of missed opportunities.

In the season opener against Philly, the Knicks trailed by two with under three minutes to go. After giving up a dunk to VJ Edgecombe, OG Anunoby committed a back-breaking turnover in the open court that allowed Tyrese Maxey to score an easy layup. The Knicks never got closer.

The Boston game that followed it felt like a fake comeback at times, but the team got within one with 1:41 to go. While the Celtics never pulled away from there, Jayson Tatum never gave them a chance to come back with tough shot after tough shot.

The inability to get a stop, coupled with a bad moving screen by Karl-Anthony Towns in a crucial spot down three with 1:11 left doomed them to an 0-2 start.

Against Orlando, the game was tied at 91 at the five-minute mark, but the Knicks played so poorly over the next four minutes that they gave themselves no chance. On their next eight possessions, the Knicks went 0-for-3 from the field, 2-for-6 from the line, and had three turnovers.

In the NBA Cup game in Philadelphia, a stepback three from Brunson gave them a 90-89 lead with 1:32 left, but things would unravel after that. A clutch LeBron fadeaway and a triple from Edgecombe on the next two possessions hurt, but so did allowing a brutal offensive rebound after getting a stop with 12 seconds left.

Their biggest strength, rebounding, failed them.

Against Cleveland on Wednesday, it was tied entering clutch time and a one-point game with 3:26 left, but a peculiar decision to continue to blitz Donovan Mitchell at halfcourt allowed the Cavs to score on essentially every trip down.

One sequence that defined this one? Deuce McBride had a fastbreak layup down by five with 35 seconds to go, got stuffed, and Peyton Watson hit Jarrett Allen on an outlet for an and-1 to ice the game.

Then, last night in Indiana, the undermanned Pacers did everything they could to throw the game away, giving the Knicks the ball down by two with 2.8 to go. They drew up a post touch for Towns, but it didn't go.

I don't hate the idea, but I really wish they used Towns' newfound playmaking ability to their advantage by trying to free up a shooter on the right wing. If I have any criticism of this team's late-game offense for years now, it's too much isolation. There was enough time for a pass to at least be an option.

The team is shooting 38.9% in the clutch, a continuation of what's been annoyingly persistent struggles with efficiency. They haven't shot enough free throws to make conclusions there, but the regression we've seen from Towns and Brunson at the line has killed them in games where every point counts.

All told, this is something you expect to eventually reverse itself, but we're getting to the point in the season where samples are getting bigger and bigger.

At some point, they have to start executing in general and consistently play as a cohesive unit for all 48 minutes.

#2694. Stark numbers show how much Falcons defense has fallen off

Every NFL season is a journey - and there's no such thing as a straight path to the Super Bowl. Even great teams go through peaks and valleys over the course of the year and have to make a lot of adjustments as they go - or else they wind up sinking fast and are out of the race by midseason.

The Altanta Falcons aren't quite there yet - if only because they're playing in a punchline of a division - but they are definitely deep into a valley right now.

Heading into today's game against the Kansas City Chiefs the Falcons have lost four of their last five games. While the one win came against the juggernaut San Francisco 49ers overall this run has featured bad play on both sides of the ball - especially their once surprisingly-tough defense that has devolved into one of the league's worst units.

Over the weekend Josh Kendall at The Athletic shared some numbers that tell the story in a painful way.

"In the first four weeks of the season, the Falcons (3-6) were second in the NFL in defensive success rate (63.9 percent), fifth in yards per play allowed (4.9), eighth in defensive EPA (6.4 per 100 snaps) and first in quarterback pressure rate (46.3 percent), according to TruMedia. Since Week 5, those numbers have plummeted to 30th (52.9), 26th (5.8), 22nd (minus-0.8) and 21st (33.7 percent)."

The Falcons were definitely punching above their weight class early in the season and they were never going to be able to sustain their early numbers against the run and rushing the passer.

Still, it's a pretty dramatic drop-off. What can we attribute this to?

The simplest answer is that they're playing tougher teams. From Weeks 1-4 they faced Pittsburgh, Carolina, Green Bay and New Orleans - and there's no heavy-hitting offense in that group except the Packers - who looked nothing like they do now back in Week 3.

Since then the Falcons have faced the Ravens, the Bears, the 49ers, the Bucs and the Bengals. Four of these five are likely playoff teams and three are potentially Super Bowl contenders if they can stay healthy.

It won't get any easier over the next couple of weeks, either. The Chiefs rank second in yardage (384.1 per game) and 10th in scoring, while the Lions rank fifth in yards (379.8) and points per game (27.8).

#2693. With trade offers rejected, Titans' plans for Tony Pollard come into question

All the factors had lined up for a Tony Pollard trade. With a trade not happening, the Titans' future plans for Pollard come into question.

At 1-7, the Titans aren't contenders. Pollard is in the final year of his contract. While a compensatory draft pick becomes possible in 2028 (depending on Tennessee's 2027 free-agent acquisitions and losses), the Titans could have gotten a draft pick in 2027 if they'd traded him.

What could they have gotten? As we hear it, they turned down a fourth-round pick and a player. (The Packers and the Broncos were the two teams linked to Pollard.)

So what's the plan for Pollard? Maybe they want to keep Pollard beyond 2026. He's in the last season of a three-year, $21.75 million deal. He's making $7.25 million this year.

And Pollard is on track for his fifth straight 1,000-yard season. He's not even in the top 20 highest-paid running backs by annual average. The franchise tag at the position for 2027 is expected to be in the range of $15 million.

Pollard will be 30 in April. That makes it harder to put a proper number on his next contract. But if the Titans weren't willing to take the best offer they could get for him, it suggests that they have a plan premised on potentially signing him to a new contract.

The decision to keep Pollard could be as simple as the Titans not being confident in their alternatives. Pollard has 147 touches in eight games. His backup, Tajae Spears, has 33 in six games. Rookie Nick Singleton has seven in four games. Julius Chestnut has no touches in eight games.

The answer could be that simple. The Titans had insufficient confidence in their other options if Pollard was traded. Which would have complicated the ongoing effort to develop quarterback Cam Ward.

Regardless, the Titans had a chance to get something now (we don't know the name of the player that was offered; it could have been a running back) and a 2027 draft pick by trading Pollard. They decided not to.

Pollard will now keep going, and quite possibly finish with more than 1,000 yards again. Which could result in Pollard leaving in 2027 — possibly with the Titans not getting a compensatory draft pick in 2028.

#2691. Galliani, ricorso FIGC: alle 11.30 udienza sulla firma del Genoa per Malagò

Il Tribunale federale valuterà la sospensiva; in discussione anche la commissione incaricata di verificare le candidature.

Il Tribunale federale nazionale della FIGC terrà alle 11.30 l’udienza sul ricorso di Adriano Galliani, candidato alla presidenza federale, che contesta la validità della candidatura dell’attuale commissario Giovanni Malagò. Secondo quanto riportato da SportMediaset, Il tribunale valuterà la richiesta di sospensiva. Al centro della controversia c’è la doppia firma del Genoa, compresa quella inizialmente concessa a Galliani e poi revocata.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La contestazione di Galliani riguarda anche la commissione voluta da Malagò per verificare le candidature alla presidenza della Federcalcio. Il reclamo dell’ex amministratore delegato del Milan è firmato dall’avvocato Filippo Lattanzi. SportMediaset riferisce che l’udienza esaminerà la sospensiva.

Il Genoa aveva prima firmato per Galliani, che aveva inserito il documento nel fascicolo della propria candidatura. Il club ha poi scelto Malagò, inviando una Pec di revoca e diffida relativa alla firma precedente.

Se la firma del Genoa non fosse ritenuta valida per Malagò, il commissario non raggiungerebbe le undici firme dei club di Serie A necessarie per candidarsi.

#2687. Branthwaite, Garner and King omitted for Spain game

England manager Thomas Tuchel has omitted Josh King, James Garner and Jarrad Branthwaite from his squad to face world champions Spain in Madrid on Sunday night.

England travelled to Spain with a squad of 26 players but Uefa rules state that only 23 players can be selected in a matchday squad for Nations League games

The return to fitness of Ezri Konsa and Djed Spence - who missed Thursday's win over Croatia - has been a contributing factor in the trio's omission.

Arsenal defender Konsa could come straight back into the starting XI while James Trafford will replace Jordan Pickford in goal.

Garner replaced Declan Rice in the 77th minute of the 2-1 victory over Croatia at Wembley, scoring a goal that was subsequently disallowed, while Everton team-mate Branthwaite and Fulham's King were unused substitutes.

King received his first senior call-up for the two final Nations League fixtures in Group A3, but the midfielder, 19, will not feature in this international window.

However, fellow first-time call-ups Jobe Bellingham and James Hill are included.

Arsenal defender Ben White, who was booed when he came on as a substitute against Croatia, is also part of the reduced 23-man party.

England sit three points behind Spain in their group and know that a win will secure top spot in the table, as they already have a superior goal difference to their opponents.

Tuchel's side are already guaranteed a quarter-final spot and a first-place finish will secure them a tie against one of the runners-ups in one of the three other groups in League A.

The two-legged quarter-finals will be played in March, with semi-finals and a final to be played in June.

In the build-up to Sunday's fixture, Tuchel reminded his players of Uefa's anti-racism protocols, with the match to be the national team's first in the Spanish capital since a notorious game at Real Madrid's Bernabeu stadium in 2004 which was overshadowed by racist chanting aimed at England players.

The Spanish federation was fined last month and ordered to play their next friendly match with a partial stadium closure after fans sang "Islamophobic and xenophobic" chants about Muslims during a game against Egypt.

"We had the same discussion before the Serbia game [in Belgrade in September 2025]," Tuchel said. "There is a protocol in place.

"Maybe we take our five minutes to inform the players about the protocol if there is a precedent [in Madrid]."

The England coach's comments were dismissed by Spain coach Luis de la Fuente, who said: "What I'd tell you, and you can tell Thomas, is that in Spain, there's no racism.

"Just like that, crystal clear. Just like in England, there's no racism in Spain. We aren't racist, any of us."

#2683. McIlroy claims season-long title on European tour for record-tying 8th time as Hatton wins in Dubai

Rory McIlroy captured the season-long Race to Dubai golf title on the European tour for a record-tying eighth time by finishing second at the World Tour Championship won by Tyrrell Hatton on Sunday.

McIlroy joined Colin Montgomerie atop the all-time list of winners of the previously named "Order of Merit" award, adding another significant achievement to the resume of a player who has done pretty much everything in golf — notably the career Grand Slam.

Hatton shot 6-under 66 to win the season-ending event by five strokes on 15-under par, but the main focus was on the tense fight for the year-long points title between McIlroy and young Spaniard Eugenio Chacarra, who arrived in Dubai atop the standings just ahead of the Northern Irishman.

In a turbulent final round in which Chacarra played one group behind McIlroy, the pair regularly exchanged places in the top two spots of the projected standings. McIlroy won out, shooting a bogey-free 67 that ultimately left him in outright second place.

Chacarra needed to eagle the par-5 18th — or get some unlikely help from a couple of players finishing their rounds after him — but neither scenario transpired. He shot 69 and tied for third, dropping him to second place in the Race to Dubai standings by the small matter of around 125 points.

McIlroy has now won the season-long title for the last five years, as well as in 2012, '14 and '15, and he was congratulated by Chacarra outside the scorer's hut.

"Deserved winner," Chacarra said, before McIlroy made a video call to his family — "stress, stress, stress," he was heard saying — and then gave an emotional TV interview in which he dedicated the title to his father, Gerry, who has been in hospital for the last two months.

"He underwent a huge operation at the end of September and is recovering well, thankfully," McIlroy said, before adding tearfully: "When I left to come to the Middle East, I went up to Cleveland to see him and he said to me, 'Go win the Race to Dubai.' This one's for him."

Chacarra said he was proud of the way he took the fight to McIlroy on his first appearance at the World Tour Championship and in his first full season on the European tour.

"I just came (up) short," the 26-year-old Chacarra said. "That's the beauty of golf."

"Obviously, when you play with the best in the world ... he's played here 14, 15 times, you knew it was going to be tough, and he had a better day than I did."

#2682. NFL tells Senator that Jalen Hurts review will include "mutually agreed-upon independent medical experts"

Five weeks since Eagles quarterback Jalen Hurts played a game in London with a blood clot in his leg, the NFL and NFL Players Association have not yet begun an official investigation of the situation.

As noted on Friday by Tim McManus of ESPN, the league and the union are still working “to hammer out [the] parameters” of the investigation.

That has not stopped the NFL from providing some preliminary information to Senator Lisa Blunt Rochester (D-Del.), who previously expressed concerns about the situation in a letter to the league.

“As outlined by the league and NFLPA’s Collective Bargaining Agreement, mutually agreed-upon independent medical experts will review the matter and share their conclusions and recommendations with both parties,” NFL executive vice president of communications, public affairs and health policy Jeff Miller said in a statement that was released by Blunt Rochester’s office on Friday.

It’s still unclear how the investigation will proceed or what it will entail. The NFLPA has not yet invoked its absolute right to launch an investigation under Article 39 of the Collective Bargaining Agreement as the communications with the league continue.

The ensuing delay is approaching the status of “head scratching.” With games being played every Thursday, Sunday, and Monday, there’s a constant risk of a similar situation arising. The sooner the Hurts situation is properly explored and any lessons learned from it are put into action, the sooner all players will benefit from any enhanced protections.

The Eagles and the league created widespread skepticism regarding whether a coverup is happening. (The NFL may not appreciate that being said, but that doesn’t make the perception inaccurate.) The NFLPA can’t afford to be viewed as being complicit in an effort to sweep the incident under the rug.

Through it all, the most effective investigative process comes from litigation, an adversarial process aimed at getting to the truth. However it happens as to Hurts, the only goal should be getting to the truth about why it all happened — and how it can be prevented from happening again.
